# First milestone

Status: implemented on 2026-11-10. `nix flake check` passes.

This document describes what the code does now.
It lists the choices that were made during the implementation and that the owner has not reviewed.
It lists the gaps between the code and the documents.
`GLOSSARY.md` defines the terms.

## Evidence

- `kerneltool test` starts the machine under QEMU and acts as the person through the QEMU monitor: keys, pointer moves, clicks.
- Each step of the scenario waits for a text in the log of the kernel. A step that does not see its text in 20 seconds fails the test.
- The scenario is a table in `tools/kerneltool/machine.odin`.
- The test saves a picture of the screen at 52 steps. `kerneltool screenshots` copies them to `docs/screenshots/`.
- `nix flake check` runs the same test in the Nix sandbox.

The scenario does these actions:

1. Wait for the kernel, the root service, the input service, the compositor and the console.
2. Type expressions and commands in the console: `1 + 2 * 3`, a variable, `length`, `hash("abc")`, a type error, `modules()`.
3. Start `life` from the console with `start("life")`. Drag its window.
4. Start `monitor` and `paint` from the menu of the bar. Choose colors and draw lines in `paint`. Start `plasma` with the launcher: the key super opens it, "pla" lists only `plasma`, Enter starts it.
5. Start `fault`. The kernel stops it on a page fault. The other programs continue.
6. Close `plasma` with its close button. Stop `paint` with `stop("paint")`.
7. Call `programs()` and `memory()`.
8. Call `gcd(84, 36)`: the console has no such command. Call `load("math")`, then `gcd(84, 36) + power(2, 10)`: the result is 1036. `square_root(1000000) + square_root(-4)` is a direct call of an export that fails: "error: square_root: the argument value has -4; it must have at least 0 (-1)", from the error record that the export wrote at the address that the console gave.
9. Call `help()`.
10. Call `isolate("probe")`: the module runs in a call host. Call `count()`, `checksum("abc" + name)`, and `stop_machine` with three handle numbers: the kernel refuses each one.
11. Call `peek(0)`: the kernel stops the call host on a page fault, and the console reports an error with the stack trace of the stop report, one line for each frame ("error: frame 1: probe + 0x...", then the frames of `host`). Call `count()`: a new call host gives 1.
12. Call `spin()`, which never returns, then type `count() + gcd(84, 36)` during the call: the console keeps the keys. Press Escape: the console closes the handle of the call host, and the kernel stops it. The line that waited then runs: an isolated call and a direct call in one expression.
13. Call `isolate("sample")`: a record and a lent handle in an isolated call. `r = Rectangle(1, 2, 30, 4)` makes a record from the description of the module; `area(r)` gives 120; `grow(r, 5)` gives "Rectangle{x = -4, y = -3, width = 40, height = 14}", a Record result. `b = buffer(1)` makes a memory object of one page. `fill(b, 3)` gives 4096 and `byte(b, 4095)` gives 3: the module wrote in the memory object of the console. `span_total(Span(b, 96, 10))` gives 30: a handle in a field of a record. `spoil(b)` gives 7 (`No_Right`): the description asks Read, and the module cannot map the object to write. `keep(b)` then `kept()` gives 2 (`Bad_Handle`): the handle ended with its call. `area(Span(b, 0, 1))` is an error of type. Then `load("sample")`: the same descriptions serve a direct call, and `g.width * g.height + keep(b) + kept()` gives 12848. Then the revocation: `hold(b)` maps the lent memory and keeps the mapping; the console revokes what it lent at the end of the call; `look()` reads through the mapping, and the kernel stops the call host on a page fault. `grants("monitor")` prints "monitor has: log, machine". `revoke("monitor", "machine")` asks the root service: the log has "root: revoked the grant machine of monitor, holdings ended: 1", and `monitor` writes "no grant to inspect the machine" in its window and in the log. A second `revoke` is refused, and a grant with an unknown name too.
14. Start `temple` from the menu of the bar.
15. Start `rings` from the menu of the bar: six programs pass records through six rings. Add three records. Stop node 4: all the records wait in the ring before it. Start node 4 again.
16. Start `threads` from the menu of the bar: one program, five threads. A thread adds the numbers to 1000 and ends; the first thread reads 500500. Four threads draw one Julia set each in the surface of the program. Key `3` stops thread 3, then starts it again. `programs()` shows 5 threads.
17. Start `workers` from the menu of the bar. Four threads add to one number for 150 ms each: with the lock no addition is lost; with no lock, millions are lost. Then four threads take 140 tiles from one queue and compute a picture.
18. Start `rights` from the menu of the bar. The program holds the machine with the right Inspect and the boot image with the right Read. It asks the kernel for nine operations. The kernel does four and refuses five with the status `No_Right`: create a device handle, stop the machine, map the boot image to write, map it to execute, give it to the compositor. The log has "rights: ok, 4 allowed, 5 refused". Start `origin` from the menu of the bar: twelve cases of the origin of a handle (copies, a chain of copies, a mapping in another program, a revocation in one program only), each with the answer of the kernel. The log has "origin: ok, 12 cases" and the measure "origin: lend a handle and take it back: N ns with 4 kernel entries; copy and close: N ns with 2".
19. Start `budget` from the menu of the bar. The program creates memory objects of 64 pages until the kernel answers `No_Budget`, then closes them: its budget has the first number of pages again. It does the same with threads. It takes 256 pages for a new budget, creates 200 pages with it, and closes the new budget: its own budget then pays for the 200 pages. The log has "budget: ok, 10 memory objects then no budget, 3 threads then no budget, each page returned". Start `limits` from the menu of the bar: three budgets with a limit of processor time (5 %, 20 % and 60 % of one processor), one thread on each that computes with no end, and a fourth thread on the budget of the program, which has the limit of 1.5 processors. The log has "limits: processor time of one second, used of limit: 49642 of 50000, 198746 of 200000, 602592 of 600000, the program 1498231 of 1500000" (the numbers change a little at each run) and "limits: ok, 4 budgets within their processor time, 40 log records written and 60 refused with 40 for each second". Press `windows` in the bar: the menu has one line for each window. Press a line: the window comes to the front, and the log has "compositor: window console in front".
20. Start `flow` from the menu of the bar. One thread writes 200 000 records in a ring and one thread reads them, four times. The log has one line for each ring: the records read in order, "0 lost", and the kernel entries of the program for one million records. The records in one second are a measure of `kerneltool measure flow`, where each ring runs for one second. On the machine of the test (QEMU with KVM, 4 processors): capacity 32 with a wait at once, 6.5 million records and 188 421 entries; capacity 1024 with a wait at once, 12.8 million and 70 774; capacity 32 with `wait_spin`, 29.2 million and 75; capacity 1024 with `wait_spin`, 29.8 million and 56. The test asserts the lines and "flow: ok, 4 rings, 0 records lost", not the numbers. Picture: `flow.png`.
21. Start `processors` from the menu of the bar. The machine of the test has four processors. Five threads of the program compute, and the program asks the kernel what each processor runs: the log has "processors: ok, 4 processors ran the program at the same time". Then 1, 2 and 4 threads compute the same picture, with no pause between the first three pictures, and the log has the three times: "processors: the picture took 404 ms with 1 thread, 198 ms with 2 threads, 148 ms with 4 threads" in one run. The test does not check the times.
   Then start `locks` from the menu of the bar: the measures of the locks of the kernel. It starts four `locker` programs; each one works only on its own objects. With 1, 2 and 4 lockers at one time, each locker enters the kernel 16 384 times (one cheap operation, `Memory_Size`, on an own memory object) and writes 2048 pages for the first time (create, map, write one byte in each of 256 pages, unmap, close). Then two threads of `locks` wake each other with the word wait 2000 times, alone, then while two lockers enter the kernel. The log has one line for each of these, with its counts ("locks: kernel entries, each locker on its own objects: 1 locker 16384, 2 lockers 32768, 4 lockers 65536"), the waits and the takes of each kind of lock for the measures with 4 lockers, and "locks: ok, the kernel counted each entry, each page returned, no wake was lost": the kernel counted each entry of each locker, each page returned to the budget of each locker, the free pages are the pages of the budgets and the reserved pages, and no wait ended at its deadline after its word changed. The test does not check a speed. Picture: `locks`. `kerneltool measure` runs the same work for a fixed time with only the desktop, and writes the kernel entries, the pages on first use and the wakes in one second.
   Then start `scheduler` from the menu of the bar: the scheduling strategies (`docs/SCHEDULING.md`). Under round robin, the fair strategy and EEVDF in turn, it runs the same cases: two threads that wake each other while a spinner runs on each processor, until the first thread did 16 turns and, under EEVDF, the kernel counted 8 turns that a wake ended; 8 spinners of one weight (the share of each); 12 spinners with the weights 256, 512 and 1024 (the share of one spinner of each weight). A case of the shares ends when each spinner ran 30 ticks, by the thread report. The wakes in one second, how late a thread that sleeps 2 ms runs, and the work in one second are measures of `kerneltool measure scheduler`. The log has one line for each strategy with these numbers, the turns that a wake ended, the moves and the time of one pick. Then a thread changes the strategy 100 times while this program starts and ends threads, two threads wake each other and 8 spinners run: "scheduler: stress: 100 changes of strategy while N threads started and stopped, ...; after the last change each thread ran: 1; thread slots N of N". The program checks what does not depend on the load of the host: no spinner got less than a sixteenth of its equal part, a wake ended turns under EEVDF and none under round robin (the counts of the kernel; how many times as often EEVDF woke is printed as a measure), the spinners of weight 1024 got more than those of weight 256 under the fair strategy and EEVDF, each thread ran after the last change, each thread slot returned, no wake was lost, the kernel refused `Strategy_Set` through a copy of the machine handle with Inspect only (`No_Right`) and a weight of 1025 (`Bad_Operation`). The log has "scheduler: ok, no spinner starved under 3 strategies, EEVDF woke N times as often as round robin (a measure), a wake ended M turns under EEVDF and 0 under round robin, ...". The keys 1 and 3 change the strategy: "scheduler: the strategy is now round robin, it was EEVDF", then back to EEVDF. Picture: `scheduler`.
22. Start `boot` from the menu of the bar. The window shows the boot display: the seven stages of the start on one line of time, the memory that the firmware gave, the machine, the modules of the boot image, the programs. The log has "boot: ok, 7 stages, N memory ranges, N programs".
23. Start `pages` from the menu of the bar. The window shows what the page tables give, as the kernel reads it from the tables: the direct map, the kernel module, the kernel stacks, and the half of the program. The log has "0 write and execute, 24 guard pages of 24", "pages: ok, the page tables keep the rules" and "pages: the processor refuses the memory of programs to the kernel". Then start `frames`: ten cases of pages on first use and of shared code, each with the answer of the kernel, read from the page table of the program. A memory object of 8192 pages has no page frame after its creation and its mapping; a read gives zero and one frame; a write to each page gives 8192 frames, each zero before. A budget of 100 pages refuses 200 pages at creation, and serves each access to 99 pages when it has 0 pages left. A program `twin` writes a page that nobody used, and `frames` reads the word. Four twins with a copy of the module each, then four with the shared code: the log has "frames: 4 twins: 320 pages with a copy of the module each, 311 pages with the shared code (3 pages of the module, once)". A fifth twin writes to its code: the kernel stops it on a page fault, and the four others still run. The log has the measures and "frames: ok, 10 cases". Picture: `frames.png`.
24. Start `paint` from the menu of the bar, and call `connect("paint")` in the console. The log has "root: connected console to the service paint" and the console draws the record `Loaded` of the connection: the module `paint`, 4 commands, the call `service`. `where(help(), "name", "paint.write")` gives the row of `paint.write` with its parameters: the console read them in the file of the program. Call `paint.line(40, 30, 440, 250, 1)`, a second line and `paint.write(150, 20, "drawn by isolated calls", 0)`: the window of `paint` shows two lines and the text, and "callers 1 calls 4". `paint.lines()` gives 2. `connect("life")`, `life.empty()`, `life.glider(10, 10)` and `life.population()` give 5: the game continues with one glider. `connect("monitor")` gives "no service runs with this name". Stop `paint`: `paint.lines()` gives "error: no service runs with the name paint". Start `paint` again: `paint.lines()` gives 0 through a new connection. Picture: `services.png`.
25. Start `log` from the menu of the bar. The window shows the records of the log: the time, the program, the channel and the text of each one, and the count of records of each channel. The log has "log: the kernel wrote 7 records in the channel boot" and "log: ok, N records, 0 lost, N channels".
26. Start `flood` from the menu of the bar. The root service starts the program and its terminal: the log has "root: started flood, with a terminal". `flood` writes lines of four runs as fast as it can. The log has "terminal: flood wrote N lines in one second, N records, with N kernel entries for one million records; the terminal drew N pictures". Page Up holds the view: the log has "terminal: the view stays at line N, flood continues". The key Space goes to the program as a record: the log has "terminal: the first key goes to flood", "flood: Space: no more lines after N", and after a second press "flood: Space: the lines continue". `stop("flood")` in the console stops the program, and its terminal stays: the log has "terminal: flood stopped with code 0 after N lines and N records". The close button of the window stops the terminal: "root: terminal stopped with code 0". The console itself is a program with a terminal: each console line of this list is typed through the terminal, as key records. Four pictures: `terminal`, `terminal_held`, `terminal_keys`, `terminal_stopped`.
27. Start `crowd` from the menu of the bar. Five runs, in which each writer writes 50 000 records: 1 writer and 1 reader, 2 and 2, 3 and 1, 1 and 3 on a shared queue, then 2 and 2 on a queue with one lock. One event starts the threads of each run. The log has, for each run, "crowd: shared queue, N writers, N readers: N written, N read, 0 wrong, N kernel entries for one million records", then "crowd: ok, 5 runs, each record written is read one time, each event started all the threads". The records in one second are a measure of `kerneltool measure crowd`. One picture: `crowd`.
28. Start `clock` from the menu of the bar. It reads the time 1 000 000 times from the time page, then 100 000 times with a kernel entry. The log has "clock: the check of the time page: 1000000 reads, 0 kernel entries", "clock: the check of a kernel entry: 100000 reads, N kernel entries" (the reads in one second are a measure of `kerneltool measure clock`), "clock: the time page and the cycle counter: in N reads the page was N to N ms behind and at most N ms ahead", then "clock: ok, the time never went back, the page and the kernel give the same time, the kernel refuses to remove the time page". One picture: `clock`.
29. Start `tally` from the menu of the bar: one module in each role. Type `connect("tally")`, then `tally.add(5, "from the console, to the service")`: the window of the program shows 5. Type `load("tally")`, then `add(2, "in the console") + add(2, "in the console")`: the console prints 6, the count of its own copy, and `tally.total()` still gives 5. Type `unload("tally")`, then `isolate("tally")`, then `add(30, "in a sandboxed program") + adds()`: the console prints 31. The three records `Loaded` have the same head of the hash. One picture: `roles`.
30. Start `manual` from the menu of the bar. The log has "manual: ok, N modules, N have their documentation". Type `connect("manual")`, then `manual.signature("tally", 1)`: the console prints "add(amount: Integer, note: Text) -> Integer". Type `manual.show("tally")`: the window shows the page of `tally`, with its documentation, the four ways to use it and its exports. The keys up and down change the module. One picture: `manual`.
31. A module with imports, and the replacement of an import. `load("lonely")` gives two lines: "error: cannot load lonely" and "error: lonely imports 06711e49774633ea..., and the load source has no file with its hash". `load("agenda")` loads agenda and its import calendar; `year_days(2000) + month_days(2, 2000)` gives 393, because calendar has a fault (2000 is not a leap year for it). `load("calendar")` then `unload("calendar")` gives a record with the memory "stays: another module imports it": one copy for the two. `replace("calendar", "calendar_changed")`: the root service writes "root: calendar_changed replaces calendar in each load that follows", and `load("agenda")` gives "error: agenda imports calendar, and its replacement calendar_changed fails the check" and "error: export days_in_month of calendar_changed: 3 parameters, calendar has 2". `replace("calendar", "agenda")` makes a cycle: "error: an import cycle: agenda imports calendar, and agenda still loads its imports" and "error: the replacement table gives agenda in place of calendar: that makes the cycle". `replace("calendar", "calendar_fixed")`, then `load("agenda")` gives the same head of the hash of agenda, and `where(loaded(), "in_place_of", "calendar")` gives the row of calendar_fixed, which agenda imports in the place of calendar; the same expression gives 395. A chain of replacements is refused. `replacements()` gives the table, and `manual.show("agenda")` shows the import and its replacement. Start `imports` from the menu of the bar: it imports calendar, and the root service gave it calendar_fixed. Its window shows twelve cases of the loader, each with the answer, and the log has "imports: ok, 12 cases" and the measures "imports: a call through an import slot: N ns to calendar, N ns to calendar_fixed in its place" and "imports: the bind of agenda: N ns, and N ns with the check of the replacement". The log also has "imports: two programs import calendar at two places: one copy of its code, 2 pages, mapped 4 times". `where(program_modules(), "replaces", "calendar")` gives the rows of calendar_fixed for the console and for `imports`. `isolate("agenda")`, then `month_days(13, 2000)` stops the call host on a bounds check. The kernel writes "in module 5d8ff6e736fda684 at offset ..., which replaces b1f1b875df9992f8", and the console prints "error: the instruction is at offset ... of calendar_fixed ..." and "error: calendar_fixed ... runs in place of calendar ...": the fault names calendar_fixed, not calendar. Two pictures: `imports`, `replacement`.
    The information of the modules (property 12). At its start `manual` writes "manual: information: 45 modules and 65 exports listed in N us; who imports calendar: 2 modules, in N us; 0 modules break a rule". The console then combines the tables that the manual serves. `columns(manual.importers("calendar_fixed"), "module name replacement slots")` prints `agenda` and `imports`, each with the import calendar and the replacement calendar_fixed, and "2 rows of Import". `columns(where(manual.running(), "module", "calendar_fixed"), "program slot module replaces")` prints the program `imports` and the hash of calendar that calendar_fixed replaces: the module records of the kernel. `sort(columns(manual.handles("Write"), "module name rights"), "name")` prints `sample`, `fill`, "Read Write". `join(columns(manual.running(), "program hash"), columns(manual.modules(), "hash name code exports"), "hash")` prints each module that runs in each program, with its bytes of code and its exports: 31 rows in one run. `manual.exports("nothing")` gives "error: manual.exports: the argument name names none of the modules (-1)", and `where(manual.modules(), "code", "large")` gives "error: where: the field code is Integer, the value is Text". `manual.overview()` shows the table of all the modules in the window: the sizes, the counts of exports and imports, who imports each module and the programs that run it. `manual.show("calendar_fixed")` shows its sizes, the two modules that import it through the replacement, and the program that runs it in place of calendar. `columns(where(manual.edges(), "replaces", "calendar"), "imported_by module replaces program")` prints the line "imports calendar_fixed calendar imports": in the program `imports`, its module imports calendar_fixed, which a replacement put in place of calendar: the module graph of what runs. Two pictures: `modules`, `module_information`.
32. Start `disk` from the menu of the bar. The root service gives the disk of the measures (a virtio block device at the place 17 of the bus) to a new block service, and the block ring to `disk`: the log has "root: started block for the device at the place 17 of the bus" and "block: ok, 8192 blocks of 4096 bytes, a queue of 256, 85 requests in flight at most, flush: yes". `disk` gives its data region to the block service through the block ring, sends 256 requests in each of five runs, then writes 128 blocks, flushes, reads them back and compares. The log has one line for each run and "disk: ok, 5 runs, 0 errors, write then read back: 0 wrong". The blocks in one second are a measure of `kerneltool measure disk`, where each run goes on for one second. In one such run, when the test still measured: read, 1 block, 1 in flight: 13 237 blocks in one second, with 2 kernel entries of `disk` and 3 of the block service for each request; 32 in flight: 165 473 blocks, 0.05 and 0.09 entries; write, 1 in flight: 11 947 blocks; 32 in flight: 111 557 blocks, 0.06 and 0.09 entries; read, 16 blocks in each request, 8 in flight: 843 424 blocks (3.3 GB) in one second. The close button stops `disk`, and the root service stops its block service: "root: block stopped with code 0". Picture: `disk`.
33. Call `connect("storage")` in the console. No storage service runs: the root service starts it, with a block service of the data disk (a new disk of 16 384 blocks), then makes the connection. The log has "root: started block for the device at the place 16 of the bus", "root: started storage", "storage: ok, a new disk: generation 1, 16384 blocks" and "root: connected console to the service storage". The connection names the private store "console", with a limit of 4 blocks. `storage.write("greeting", "kept on the disk")` gives 2, the generation of the commit; `storage.read("greeting")` gives "kept on the disk". `storage.write("second", "one block more")` fails: "error: storage.write: the call needs 5 blocks in all; the limit is 4 blocks (-2)". `storage.write("", "x")` gives "error: storage.write: the argument name is empty; it must have 1 to 39 bytes (-3)", and `storage.read("nothing")` gives "error: storage.read: the argument name: the store has no object with this name (-7)": the sentences come from the error record of each call. `storage.objects()` gives "greeting 16 bytes, generation 2". The console acts for the principal: `storage.stores()` gives "generation 2, 4 of 16384 blocks in use; console: 1 objects, 3 of 4 blocks, 1 connection". `grants("console")` gives "console has: log, machine, image, store"; `revoke("console", "store")` writes "root: revoked the grant store of console, connections changed: 1"; then `storage.write("greeting", "changed")` gives "error: storage.write: the caller has no grant for this: a private store (-1)", `storage.stores()` shows the store with "0 connections", and `grants("console")` gives "console lost: store". Picture: `storage`.
34. Start `errors` from the menu of the bar: 19 cases, each one an error made for real, with the sentence of its error record under it. Ten kernel operations (a right that lacks, a copy that was revoked, a handle that was closed, a handle of another kind, a number that names no handle, 64 pages from a budget of 8, a log text in the kernel half, a program name of 40 bytes, an unknown flag, the operation 99), two loads (a missing import, a replacement that fails its check), a direct call of `math.square_root(-4)`, five isolated calls of the storage service (an empty name, a name of 40 bytes, a zero byte, a write over the limit of the store, an absent object), and the program `fault`, which faults three calls deep. The log has one line for each case, for example "errors: b use a copy after its revocation: Memory_Size: the argument memory, handle 0xa, was revoked", then "errors: ok, 19 cases, each with the expected error record". The tool reads the stack trace "errors: stack of fault: fault + 0x1444, fault + 0x15d9, fault + 0x15f9, fault + 0x1656" and names each frame from the linked file of `fault`: `fault::three fault::two fault::one entry`. The key `b` runs case b again. The log has the measures "errors: a kernel operation that succeeds: N ns; one that fails, with its error record: N ns; a direct call that succeeds: N ns; one that fails: N ns". Picture: `errors`.
34b. Start `interface_elements` from the menu of the bar: the log has the measures of the damage by difference ("interface_elements: nothing changed: 68 records, 0 damage rectangles, ...", "one number changed", "everything moved", "ok, the whole frame drawn in ... us; the next frame drawn in its ... damage rectangles in ... us"). Tab and Enter press its button ("the button was pressed 1 times"), Tab and the arrow down move its list ("the list chooses clock"), Tab and "hello" fill its text input ("the text is \"hello\""). Pictures: `interface_elements`, and `interface_damage` after its button "show the damage".  At its start the compositor writes the measures of `copy_bytes_without_overlap` and `fill_bytes_with_value`; `clock` and `remote` write the time of one picture and the records and damage of the interface package.
34c. The text of each window of this test is the vector font (`docs/FONTS.md`): JetBrains Mono from the glyph cache of the boot image, with subpixel bands, at the scales 1, 2 and 3. At start the root service writes "root: the resource glyph_cache, 390624 bytes, its hash checked in ... us"; the boot display is drawn with it (the picture `boot_fails` is drawn by the root service). Start `vector_glyphs`: "vector_glyphs: one glyph from its outline, in fixed point, with the stripes (the least of batches): scale 1 ... ns, scale 2 ... ns, scale 3 ... ns; from the glyph cache: ... ns; Spleen 8x16: ... ns", and one line for each of the six candidate fonts ("the cache of ...: 95 characters at 3 scales built in ... us, ... bytes"). Picture: `font_candidates`. Start `settings`: "settings: ok, font vector, subpixel order rgb"; picture `font_vector` with the console, `settings` and `monitor`. In the console, `connect("settings")` then `settings.set("font", "spleen")`: the compositor writes "compositor: the settings changed: font spleen, subpixel order rgb; N windows draw again", each window draws again in Spleen 8x16 ("settings: drawn with the font spleen, ..."); picture `font_spleen`. From the host: `settings.get("font")` gives "spleen"; `settings.set("font", "vector")` and `settings.set("subpixel_order", "bgr")` give 1 and the compositor writes each change; `settings.get("subpixel_order")` gives "bgr"; `settings.set("font", "comic")` gives the error record -3 and `settings.set("colour", "red")` -2; `settings.set("subpixel_order", "rgb")` gives 1. Picture: `settings`. Host tests: `coverage_is_the_area`, `the_last_column`, `hostile_files`, `filters_measured`, `golden_glyph_cache`, `damaged_glyph_cache` (`support/vector_font`), `blend_error_against_srgb`, `text_without_cache_is_spleen`, `glyph_draw_cost` (`support/graphics`).
35. Call `memory()`. The console draws the record `Memory`: the pages in use, the reserved pages (paid, with no page frame yet), the pages of the budgets, and `free_pages_no_budget_holds`, which is 0: after all the starts and stops of the test, each free page is in a budget or reserved. The record also has the number of processors; `locks()` gives, for each kind of lock of the kernel, the times that a processor took it and the times that it waited.
36. Call `shutdown()`. The machine stops with code 0.
37. A second machine starts with a boot image that has no compositor. The root service cannot start the compositor: the boot display stays on the screen with "the start failed: cannot start the compositor: status 1", and the machine does not stop. The log has "root: the boot display shows the failure". The test runs this machine first.
38. Eight more machines start with one data disk, new before the first one (the test of persistence, `scenario_persist_*`). First machine: start `notes` from the menu of the bar; the root service starts the storage service and connects `notes` to its private store "notes": "notes: a new store: no note, the count 0". Type "kept across a restart": each key is one commit. Press Tab: `notes` counts 1000, one commit for each step: "notes: count 1000, stopped: 1000 commits in 3224 ms". `shutdown()` stops the machine. Second machine: start `notes`: "storage: ok, generation 1022: 1 stores, 2 objects, 9 blocks in use, 7 blocks checked, 0 wrong" and "notes: restored the note "kept across a restart" and the count 1000, its 3 blocks agree". Picture: `notes`. Press Tab, and the tool ends QEMU (`quit`) after "notes: count 1100", while the commits continue: the machine does not stop. Third machine: the store has 0 wrong blocks, and "notes: restored ... the count 1106, its 3 blocks agree, the last run stopped before its end" (the count changes from run to run). Then the tool writes zero bytes over the second half of the root block with the highest generation, as a write that a stop cut. Fourth machine: "storage: the root of generation 1128 has a wrong check: a write that a stop cut; the service uses the root before it", "storage: ok, generation 1127", and the count of the commit before, 1105, with its 3 blocks in agreement. Then the tool changes one byte of the first block of data of the note. Fifth machine: "storage: 1 blocks have a wrong check: 1 objects and 0 stores are damaged; the service serves the others", the count is restored, and `notes` prints "WRONG: the storage service cannot give the note: the object is damaged" (picture `notes_damaged`); the keys "again" write a new note. Sixth machine: "0 wrong", and the note "again". Then the tool changes one byte of the block of data of the note that only the newest root names. Seventh machine: "storage: the newest root names a block with a wrong check, and the root before it names none: the root before it is used", and the note of the commit before, "agai". Then the tool makes a new disk with half of a first root. Eighth machine: "storage: the first root of the disk has a wrong check and no commit followed it", then a new disk.
38b. Two more machines test a loss of power (`power_loss.odin`, `kerneltool test power`). The kernel tool serves the data disk to QEMU itself, with the Network Block Device protocol, so it knows each write and each flush, in order. First machine, on a new disk: `notes` keeps the note "kept through a loss of power", then counts 1000 commits; after "notes: count 300" and a seeded count of writes (seed 20261111), the disk loses its power at the write of a root: it answers nothing more, and the tool ends QEMU. "the disk received 737 writes, 736 flushes and 378 reads; 1 writes came after the last flush". The tool then builds the images that a real disk can hold (the durable image, each prefix and each single write of the writes after the last flush, each of them torn after 1 sector, half of its sectors or all but one, and 300 seeded random subsets with torn writes) and checks each one with the code that the storage service runs at its start (`support/storage_mount`, on the host): "306 images of the disk after the loss of power, 72 of them with a torn write, each one checked with the code of the storage service on the host in 1162 ms: 227 give the durable commit, 79 the commit in flight, none a mix". "367 durable commits, and 736 flushes reached the disk: the block service passes each flush of the storage service to the device". Second machine, on the hard image (each write after the last flush lost, and the write of the root torn): "storage: the root of generation 369 has a wrong check: a write that a stop cut; the service uses the root before it", "storage: ok, generation 368", "notes: restored the note "kept through a loss of power" and the count 339, its 3 blocks agree". Picture: `power_loss`.
38c. The error records of the other calls (`MODULE_CONTRACT.md`, section 8, "A ring completion" and "The other calls"). Each family has a case of `errors` (the second page, picture `errors_families`): "v a wrapper: display.open of 0 by 100 pixels: display.open: the argument width has the value 0, which the call refuses; it takes at most 2048", "w a wrapper: terminal.open with no terminal: terminal.open: the caller has no grant for this: a terminal", "x a ring: root.Connect("storage"), not callable: root.Connect: the caller has no grant for this: a connection to this service", "y an export: tally.add, a note of 40 bytes: tally.add: the argument note has 40 bytes; it can have at most 36 bytes", "errors: ok, 25 cases". The block ring: "disk: a read of 2 blocks from the last block: block.Read: the argument first reaches 8193 blocks, past the end: there are 8192 blocks". The datagram ring: "net: UDP to 10.0.2.2 port 9: refused: datagram.Send: the argument address, 10.0.2.2 port 9, is refused: the grant names 10.0.2.2 port 7 only", "net: UDP of 2000 bytes: refused, no fragment: datagram.Send: the argument count has 2000 bytes; it can have at most 1472 bytes", and after the revocation "... is refused: the grant names no remote (it was revoked, or the remote is not admitted)". The root ring, from the console: "error: root.Start: the argument name names none of the modules", "error: root.Stop: the argument name names a program of the system: it starts at boot, or it is a device service, and the call does not act on it", "error: root.Revoke: the argument name names none of the programs", "error: cannot connect to plasma: root.Connect: the argument name names none of the services that run". The exports: "error: paint.line: the argument color has the value 9, which the call refuses; it takes at most 5", "error: add: the argument amount has -6; it must have at least 0". The measures of one failed call of each family, before and after (`errors` and `net`, one machine run each): a wrapper (`terminal.open` with no terminal) 0.4 ns, then 5.4 ns: the record is 56 bytes that it writes; a command of the root ring (`Connect` refused, the round trip) 21226 ns, then 20556 ns: the record is lost in the round trip; an export (`tally.add` with a note of 40 bytes, direct) 50.6 ns when it took the note and cut it, then 3.1 ns when it refuses it with its record; the datagram ring (a Send of 2000 bytes, Too_Large, the round trip) 1173 ns, then 1062 ns: seven values where there were two.
38d. Versions in the storage service (property 9; design in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, U5, "Versions"). The main machine: `notes` commits each key, and the storage service keeps the state before each commit: "notes: the note is "versions", generation 11, 7 versions; one commit of the note: 3778 us in the mean of 8"; Up twice: "notes: the version 7 of 8, generation 10: "version"", "notes: the version 6 of 8, generation 9: "versio"" (picture `notes_versions`: the bar has one mark for each version, the one shown is lit); Enter: "notes: restored the version of generation 9: "versio", in the commit of generation 12 in 5798 us; the note before it is a version". From the host (`Call`, through the remote service that acts for the principal): `storage.versions("notes", "note")` gives "9 rows of Version" (generation, time, date, bytes, own_blocks, current); `storage.version("notes", "note", 7)` gives "vers"; `storage.compare("notes", "note", 6, 11)` gives one range, first 3, count 5; `storage.version("notes", "note", 99)` gives "error: storage.version: the argument generation: the object has no version of the generation 99 (-9)"; `restore` gives the commit 13 and `clone("notes", "note", "copy")` the commit 14. The console: "notes: 2 objects, 9 versions, 19 of 4500 blocks, 2 shared, 1 connection" (picture `storage_versions`: the clone shares its list and its block). The limit of a store: the console has 4 blocks; "storage.write("greeting", "changed at the limit")" gives the commit 3 and "storage: the store console is at its limit of 4 blocks: the commit of generation 3 thinned 1 version, the oldest of the store first"; a change that the current objects alone cannot hold: "error: storage.write: the call needs 5 blocks in all, with no earlier version kept; the limit is 4 blocks (-2)". A list of 1000 versions (the count, after 1000 commits): "notes: the versions of the count: 1000 rows in 50544 us". The two losses of power (`kerneltool test power`, the crash search of 38b with each version checked byte for byte): before the limit, "306 images ... in 2456 ms: 227 give the durable commit, 79 the commit in flight, none a mix; each object and each of its versions byte for byte (339 versions durable, 340 in flight, 0 thinned by the commit in flight)"; during a thinning at the limit of 4500 blocks, "306 images ... in 6385 ms ... (1119 versions durable, 1119 in flight, 1 thinned by the commit in flight)"; then "storage: ok, generation 1247: 1 stores, 2 objects, 1119 versions, 4500 blocks in use". The retention rule: 6 host tests (`odin test support/storage_retention`).
38e. The date. The program `date` reads the real-time clock at its start: "date: ok, 2026-11-11 16:45:42 UTC, seconds since 1970 1794415542, from the real-time clock at the time 638 ms of the system; the date of the time 0 in 598085 us" (it waits for the clock to change its second). The tool compares the date of the machine with the clock of the host (QEMU: `-rtc base=utc`): at the start "the date of the machine ... is 1794416273 s since 1970, the host gives 1794416273: 0 s apart"; about five minutes later, from the host, `date.now()` gives "Date{milliseconds = 1794416596428, year = 2026, month = 11, day = 11, hour = 17, minute = 3, second = 16, weekday = 3}", "3 s apart" (the date advanced with the host; the tolerance is 5 s). The HTTP server sends the field Date, which the TCP of the host reads: "the field Date "Wed, 11 Nov 2026 17:14:55 GMT" of the answer to / ... 1 s apart", the same for /large and /sink. The versions have the date of their commit: "notes: the version 7 of 8, generation 10, 2026-11-11 17:03:15: "version"". The bar shows the date and the time of day in UTC in each picture. The calendar: 4 host tests (`odin test support/calendar`), with each day from 1900 to 2500.
39. Six more machines start, each with one test of the kernel on itself. The kernel does one thing that its page tables must refuse: it uses more than its stack, it writes to its code, it writes to its code through the direct map, it executes its data, it reads the memory of a program outside a copy, it executes the code of a program. Each time the processor refuses, the kernel reports the fault with its reason ("the kernel stack of the processor is full", "write to a read-only page", ...), and the machine stops with code 2.
40. One more machine starts for the network (`scenario_network`). Its virtio network device sends each frame as one UDP datagram to the peer of the tool, on the loopback of the host: the peer is the gateway 10.0.2.2 and an echo server on port 7. Start `net` from the menu of the bar. The root service starts the frame service of the device at the place 18 of the bus, then the network service, then `net` with one endpoint: "frame: ok, the hardware address 52:54:00:12:34:56, the link is up", "network: an endpoint for net: the local port 49152, the grant 10.0.2.2 port 7". `net` shows twelve cases, each with its answer: the address 10.0.2.15/24 and the link; ARP: the gateway answers 52:55:0a:00:02:02; an echo of the gateway, in 192 us in one run; a datagram of 41 bytes to port 7 and the same 41 bytes back; a datagram to port 9, refused with "the grant gives 10.0.2.2 port 7 only"; an echo of 10.0.2.3, refused; a datagram of 2000 bytes, refused with the limit 1480 (a datagram with its UDP header); a datagram that `net` wrote with the source port 1234, refused by the network service: "refused: the source port 1234 of the header is not the local port of this endpoint, 49152". The peer sent 11 malformed frames before its first answer. One has a wrong UDP checksum: the network service computes no checksum of a datagram, so it gives that one to the endpoint of `net`, and `net` drops it: "dropped by this program: 1 datagram; the network service gave it to the endpoint of its port". One came to the local port of `net` from the remote port 9, that its grant does not name: a row of this endpoint, "refused: from port 9; a row of this endpoint". Nine have no endpoint (a short frame, IPv6, a wrong IPv4 checksum, a header of 16 bytes, a total length after the frame, a fragment, a UDP length after the packet, a port of no endpoint, ARP with a hardware address of 8 bytes): rows of the machine, that `net` reads because its grant has the right Inspect: "9 refused in 9 kinds, 9 of 9 expected; no fault". The log has the sentence of each Problem. The network service answered the echo request of the peer. The log has "net: ok, 12 cases" and the measures: "net: the header and the checksum of a datagram of 1472 bytes: N ns, in this program (no offload)", "net: the round trip of one datagram of 64 bytes: 60 us at least, 83 us in the middle, over 1000", "net: a burst with 64 datagrams in flight: 81733 datagrams in one second, sent and answered, 0 lost", "net: kernel entries for each datagram: 0.21 of this program, 0.51 of the network service, 0.98 of the frame service" (one run). Picture: `network`. Then `grants("net")` gives "net has: log, machine, network", `revoke("net", "network")` gives "root: revoked the grant network of net, endpoints changed: 1", and a key in `net` sends one more datagram: "refused: the endpoint has no grant now". The close button stops `net`, its endpoint ends, and the network service writes its passes over the bytes of the datagrams of `net`: "network: the endpoint of net, UDP: this service read each payload byte one time on receive and one time on send, for the copy, and computed no checksum of it: as it must". Then `memory()` gives "budgets N pages: all the free pages". After the machine stops, the tool checks the peer: each frame from the machine was correct (the peer reads the frames with its own code), and the machine answered its echo request: "the peer received 60659 frames from the network service, 0 wrong" in one run. The firmware also uses the device before the kernel starts: the peer reads strictly from the first ARP frame of 10.0.2.15, and counts the frames before it. At its end the person closes `remote` and `manual`, the launcher starts `life` and `plasma`, and the tool places four windows: the picture `desktop`. Then the launcher lists 6 names for "p", the names that start with "p" first; the arrow down chooses `plasma` (the picture `launcher`); Escape closes it. "xyz" lists 0 names, and Enter starts nothing (the picture `launcher_empty`); the key super closes it.
41. On the same machine, calls from the host (`MODULE_CONTRACT.md`, section 4, "A call from the host"). Start `monitor`, `manual`, then `remote` from the menu of the bar. The root service gives `remote` an endpoint whose grant names 10.0.2.2 port 7100, and a ring that serves Connect only to `manual`, `monitor` and `storage`: "remote: ok, an endpoint with the grant 10.0.2.2 port 7100". The peer sends six malformed call requests, and `remote` refuses each one with the sentence of its error record: "remote: the call has 20 bytes; it must have at least 112 bytes", an unknown kind, more bytes of arguments than the datagram has, a result past the end of the call region, an error record that is not aligned, and a service that the root service does not let it call ("the caller has no grant for this: a connection to this service"). Then the steps `Call` of the tool: `monitor.count("processors")` gives the Integer 4; `monitor.programs()` gives a table of 10 rows of `Program` with the row of `remote`, and the monitor wrote "monitor: programs: 10 rows"; `monitor.counts()` gives 13 rows of `Count`; `monitor.served()` gives the Record `Served{calls = ..., callers = ..., pictures = ...}`; `manual.modules()` gives 52 rows of `Module` in 9 datagrams; `storage.stores()` gives the Text "generation 2, 5 of 16384 blocks in use; console: 1 objects, 3 of 4 blocks, 0 connections" (the root service starts the storage service at the first connection). Failures give the sentence of the error record of the service: "error: monitor.count: the argument name names nothing that exists (-3)", "error: storage.size: the caller has no grant for this: a private store (-1)", "error: manual.exports: the argument name names none of the modules (-1)". `monitor.count(4)` is refused on the host, before a datagram: "the parameter name of monitor.count is a Text, the argument is an Integer". The tool measures a small call (`monitor.count`) from the host: in the last full check, a round trip of 181 us at least and 191 us in the middle over 1000 calls, and 5088 calls in one second one after the other (an earlier run: 172 us, 202 us and 4734 calls). The round trip of a datagram of `net` in the same machine is 52 to 60 us at least: a call adds the isolated call from `remote` to the service, and on the host the socket of the tool and the thread of the peer. The window of `remote` drew one picture in 119 us. `grants("remote")` gives "remote has: log, network, acts_for_principal"; `revoke("remote", "acts_for_principal")` gives "root: revoked the grant acts_for_principal of remote, connections changed: 3", and then `monitor.programs()` gives "error: monitor.programs: the caller has no grant for this: to act for the principal (-1)", `manual.running()` the same (-3), and `storage.stores()` "no grant to see the stores"; `manual.modules()` still works. Picture: `remote`. After the machine stops, the tool checks that `remote` refused each malformed request with the expected field and kind. The second layer (the network machine): the peer drops the datagram of the answer of `monitor.count("processors")`; the tool sends the request 2 times, "remote: the request ... came again: its answer went again, and the call did not run again; 1 in all", and `monitor.served()` counts 2 calls across the step (the call and its own). A request with a changed hash: "error: the machine runs monitor <hash>; the request named <hash>". `compositor.pointer()` and `compositor.windows()` give the place of the pointer and the windows; after `revoke("remote", "acts_for_principal")` the compositor refuses them, and the tool moves the pointer with no confirmation again. `monitor.counts()` gives 15 rows, with the scheduling strategy.
42. The main machine launches `remote` after `workers` (a new window before that moves the places of the windows that the steps press at fixed places). From then on the tool reads `compositor.pointer()` before each press and moves the pointer again until it is the place of the step: in the last full check, 120 places confirmed on the main machine, 0 after a correction, one confirmation 257 us at least and 299 us in the middle (2 places on the network machine: 305 and 350 us); 3.7 ms before the tool kept the module files, when it read and hashed the file of the compositor at each call. The calls per second did not change: a small call took 173 us at least and 190 us in the middle, 5128 calls in one second (5088 in the first layer). `log.records(0)` prints the log records as a table, and 11 checks of the scenario are steps `Record`: the program, the channel and the level of the record, and a part of its text, for example `root`, `devices`, `info`, "root: started block for the device at the place 17 of the bus".
43. A developer tool (`docs/TOOLS.md`). At the end of the main machine, while `errors` still holds the program `fault` that stopped, the scenario starts `monitor` again (its machine grant was revoked above) and launches `inspect`. The letters "threads" choose the program `threads`: "inspect: threads: 18 mappings, 327 mapped pages, 204 with a frame, 5 threads, 1 modules; the kernel says 327 mapped pages and 5 threads", "inspect: one sample of threads took 102 us and 68 kernel entries". The step `Agree` checks that `inspect.mappings("threads")` and `inspect.threads("threads")` give the mapped pages and the threads that `monitor.programs()` gives: 327 pages in 18 mappings and 5 threads; for `console`, 2241 pages in 24 mappings and 2 threads. Picture `inspect`: the address space of `console` as a band (module code, constants and data, shared rings and regions, the boot image as device memory, the module `math` that the console loaded, the thread areas), the table of the mappings with the pages that have a frame, the modules, and the two threads with their state, home queue, weight, request, lag and processor time. Space freezes the sample and starts it again. The letters "fault" choose the stopped program: "inspect: fault stopped: page fault at 0x0; stack: fault + 0x1454, fault + 0x1479, fault + 0x1499, fault + 0x16c6, near entry + 0x46"; picture `inspect_stopped`. Tab shows the bytes of the memory of `inspect`: the code of its export `entry`, and four pages of a memory object of which two were written; the two others have no page frame and the tool does not read them (picture `inspect_hex`). From the host: `inspect.mappings("console")` prints 24 rows of `Mapping` with "console + offset", the kind, the rights, the memory object and its names; `inspect.threads("threads")`, `inspect.modules("console")`; `inspect.mappings("nothing")` gives "error: inspect.mappings: the argument program names none of the programs (-2)".
43. One more machine starts for TCP (`scenario_tcp`, `docs/NETWORK.md`), with the peer of the tool on `-netdev dgram`. Start `server` from the menu of the bar. The root service gives it a segment endpoint with a listen grant: "network: an endpoint for server: TCP, the listen port 80, the remotes that send to it". The step `Peer` asks the peer for one case at a time; the peer is a TCP client of `server` with `support/tcp`, and it changes the frames with a generator that the case seeds. The cases: a page and a close from the peer ("closed, the remote first"); 1 MiB of `/large` from the server and 1 MiB to `/sink`, each checked by its FNV-1a hash, with a close from the server ("closed, this server first"); the two again with 5 % of the frames dropped in each direction, with 10 % reordered, with 10 % sent two times; a window of the peer at 0 for 1.5 s ("4 probes"); a RST of the peer after 100 KiB ("reset by the remote"); an ACK of no connection, which gets a RST; a RST in the window and a SYN on a connection, which get challenge ACKs, and the connection serves one more request ("2 challenges"); 16 connections at once ("server: 16 connections open at once"). `grants("server")` gives "server has: log, network". The close button stops `server`, the endpoint ends, and `memory()` finds each page. The peer checks each frame of the machine with its own code, also the TCP checksum: "0 wrong". Picture: `server`.
44. One more machine starts for TCP against a stack that this project did not write (`scenario_tcp_real`): QEMU user networking, whose TCP is the one of libslirp (from 4.4BSD). The step `Fetch` makes the TCP of the host fetch `/`, `/large` and `/sink` of `server` through `hostfwd` (a port of the loopback of the host to the port 80 of the machine), and checks the hash: "the TCP of the host fetched /large from server through libslirp: 1048576 bytes, fnv1a64 e7867887f2822325 as expected". Then `start("fetch")` in the console: `fetch` has a client grant of 10.0.2.100 port 80, which `guestfwd` gives to a listener of the tool. Its eight cases: the grant; a SYN to 10.0.2.2, refused by the network service with the grant; a SYN from a local port out of its block, refused; the handshake; `GET /hello`; `GET /large`, 1 MiB with its hash; `POST /sink`, 1 MiB, and the listener gives the same hash; the close from this side. The log has "fetch: ok, 8 cases". Pictures: `fetch`, `server_real`.
45. On the machine with libslirp, after `fetch`: `connect("server")`, then `server.put("hello.txt", "kept in the store of server")`: the log has "server: the page /hello.txt is in the store: 27 bytes", and the TCP of the host fetches `/hello.txt` with the same 27 bytes. `server.pages()` lists it. Then the step `Http` runs HTTP/1.1 from the TCP of the host (`tools/kerneltool/http_host.odin`): 1000 requests on one connection (the log has "server: 1000 requests on one connection of 10.0.2.2 port N in N ms: N requests in one second"); 300 requests with a new connection each; three requests in one segment, with three answers in order (GET / 200, HEAD /counts.json 200 with no body, GET /nothing 404); a head in 2 sends and a body of 200 000 bytes in 10 sends 20 ms apart, with the same hash back; HEAD / then a request on the same connection; 16 connections at once; each refusal with its status (no Host 400, a bad request line 400, PUT 405, DELETE 405, 404, a target of 1100 bytes 414, 71 fields 431, a body over 16 MiB 413, an unknown method 501, a chunked body 501, HTTP/2.0 505, half a head 408 after 10 s). `server.remove("hello.txt")`, then `/hello.txt` gives 404. Picture: `server_real` (both tables of the window live).
46. The pointer pictures (`docs/POINTERS.md`). The build converts the theme of the owner, Posy's Cursor, from the Xcursor folder of the flake input `posy-cursors` (`kerneltool pointers`: "pointers_posy: 15 shapes, 178 pictures, 883008 bytes, from resources/pointers/posy.manifest in 34.4 ms"), and the theme of the system from the grids of `resources/pointers/system.manifest` (42 pictures, 208 512 bytes, 0.7 ms); both are resources of the boot image. The root service checks the hash of the resource and gives it read-only to the compositor: "root: the resource pointers_posy (883008 bytes, its hash checked in 1810 us) is given to compositor, read-only". The compositor validates it once: "compositor: the pointer theme Posy's Cursor (CC-BY-NC-4.0): 15 shapes, 178 pictures, 883008 bytes, validated in 653 us", and measures its blend: "the blend of the arrow of 32x32 (860 of 1024 pixels transparent): 965 ns; a copy of the same size: 153 ns", and the pointer in 256 frames: "2040 ns for a frame in the mean". The terminal asks for the shape `text` over its surface. At the end of the main machine the scenario starts `pointers`, and 11 steps `Point` move the pointer with no press and read the shape in `compositor.pointer()`: `move` over the title bar of `pointers`, `pointing_hand` over its close button, `arrow` over its border, `wait` over the tile of `wait` (the request of the program: "compositor: pointers asks for the pointer shape wait over its surface"; an animated shape of 77 frames), `arrow` and `move` over two other tiles; a press on the second button shows the theme of the system, and `help` over its tile; then `pointing_hand` over the button of the programs in the bar, `arrow` over the bar, `text` over the surface of the console and over the input line of the launcher. Pictures: `pointers` (the theme of the owner, the pointer `wait` of Posy over its tile), `pointers_system` (the theme of the system, the pointer `help`), `pointer_text` (the shape `text` over the console). `kerneltool check` builds the two theme files two times: "62 modules and 2 pointer theme files are the same bytes after a second build". The first `nix flake check` of the branch ran each machine test and then failed on "`console.module` / `root.module` is not the same bytes after a second build", the fault that choice 359 corrects; the second one passed. The numbers are from one run with a load of about 30 on the host, the measures of speed of this run are not valid (rule of the brief: a load above 8).
47. An independent read of the HTTP server (`/tmp/review_http.md`, 2026-11-11): each finding has a test that failed on the code before (a scratch build of the old code with the new tests, which went on after each failure). On the machine of the peer: "one byte after a request" (B1: the server ends it after 10 s with 408 and its FIN), "answers then close to a zero window" (F3: a RST after 10 s), "body then nothing" and "slow reader" (W6: a RST, the log has "aborted: no byte of the body in 10 s" and "aborted: an answer under 4096 bytes in one second"), "page changed during its answer" (B2: 40 pipelined answers of a page of 3200 bytes; the console removes the page with `server.remove`; the peer reads only after that; the log has "aborted: the page changed during its answer" and a page comes after it: before, the program stopped at a bounds check). On the machine with libslirp: "body-not-taken" (F4: one answer, then the end), "requests-then-fin" (W7: three answers), "headers" (nosniff, and `Connection: keep-alive` for HTTP/1.0), the line of the ends of connections not on the log (W10), and after `revoke("server", "store")` a `put` and a `remove` that the store refuses while `/kept.txt` keeps its bytes (F5, W8). Host tests: the absolute form, HTTP/1.2 and empty lines before the request line (`support/web`), the entries of the page index and the escape of HTML, the place of a remote at 60 s and at 120 s (`support/network`). The case `leak` sends 192 ports of each kind: with 64 it passed on the old service (a scratch run).
48. Randomness (`docs/RANDOMNESS.md`). `kerneltool test random` starts three machines. Two have the processor of the host: the log has "root: the random seed of the system, strength entropy (rdseed): rdseed present, 8 good values, 0 refused; rdrand present, 8 good, 0 refused". One has `-cpu max,-rdrand,-rdseed`: the log has "strength weak (the cycle counter only): rdseed absent ...", and each program still starts. The program `randomness` shows the facts of the seed and its own generator (pictures `randomness` and `randomness_weak`). The first 16 bytes of the stream of 4 programs in the 3 machines all differ (the steps `Remember` and `Distinct`). `kerneltool cryptography-origin` (in `check`) proves that the 4 files of ChaCha20 in `support/cryptography` are their origin in Odin `dev-2026-05` with the listed import changes only; the RFC 8439 vectors pass on the host.
49. Tables and bytes as parameters (`MODULE_CONTRACT.md`, "Tables and bytes as parameters"). In the console, after `load("sample")`: `t = readings(4)`, `sum_values(t)` gives 100, `scale_values(t, 7)` changes the rows of the variable in place (`t` prints 70, 140, 210, 280), `sum_values(where(t, "sensor", 2))` gives 210, and `sum_values(columns(t, "sensor value"))` gives "error: sum_values: argument 1 is Table(Reading, Read), not Table(Reading)"; after `isolate("sample")` the same rows go into the call region and `scale_values(t, 3)` then `sum_values(t)` gives 300. `manual.signature("sample", 15)` gives "sum_words(words: Table(Word, Change), text: Bytes(Read)) -> Integer". From the host: `kerneltool call "monitor.counts_named([(\"programs\"), (\"processors\")])"` gives 2 rows of Count, a Change table is refused with its sentence, and `sample` is refused: `remote` cannot reach it. The program `tables` calls 8 cases in three ways with the same arguments: a direct call of `sample` loaded in its memory, a call host of `sample`, and the service `sample` (the same module, which the root service starts as a program): each answer is the expected one, also the guard after the capacity of an Append table, which an export that writes past its capacity overwrites in a direct call only. Five hostile cases through the call host, and the measures of 64, 1024 and 65536 records of 40 bytes. The program `interface_through_module` builds one frame through the module `interface_module` and with the package compiled in: the two draw tables are equal. Pictures: `tables`, `table_argument`, `manual_tables`, `interface_module`.
50. The module `cryptography` (`docs/CRYPTOGRAPHY.md`). `kerneltool cryptography-origin` (in `check`) proves that the 37 taken files are their origin in Odin `dev-2026-05` with the 6 listed import changes only. `odin test support/cryptography/vectors` runs the vectors of RFC 8439, FIPS 180-4, RFC 4231, RFC 5869, RFC 7748, RFC 8032 and the GCM test case 4 on the host. `kerneltool test crypto` starts `cryptography_vectors`, which imports the module and runs the same vectors through its exports on the machine: the log has "ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439 2.8.2): passed; a changed byte gives Not_Authentic" and the same for AES-128-GCM, X25519 and Ed25519, then "7 of 7 vectors passed" with the BLAKE3 hash of the module. Picture: `cryptography_vectors`.
51. The line of the console (`docs/CONSOLE.md`). At the end of the main machine, the person types `mcnt` in the console: the completion list opens while the keys come, Escape closes it, Tab opens it again, and the log has "console: the completion list opens by Tab: N names of M match \"mcnt\", the first is monitor.count; one ranking takes N us at most, over K keys" (picture `console_completion`). Enter takes the first name with its `(`: "console: the completion gave the line monitor.count(". The caret: `"acdf"`, then Home, Right, Right, `b`, End, Left, Left, Delete, Backspace, `e`, Enter prints `abce`. The history search: two lines with `length(`, Control-R, `leng`, Control-R, Control-S, Control-R, Enter: "console: the history search gave the line length(\"first search line\")", and a second Enter runs it (picture `console_history_search`). The host tests of `support/line_editor` pin each key, a full line, an empty line, the completion places, the call at the caret, the order of five queries over real names, 10 000 candidates, hostile names and a hostile history record. The measure of one key with the list open: `measure_completion()` writes "console: one key with the completion list: the collect N us, the rank N us, the rows N us, over K names" for the names of the console and for 512 names of 128 bytes, the least of 20 batches of 10 keys, in the main machine and in `kerneltool measure`. The host tests of `support/line_editor` also pin the line history file (a round trip, the newest lines in one block, ten hostile files), and those of `support/terminal` the form of a hint line for each width. The line history across a stop: the first machine of persistence types `length("kept across a stop")` and `shutdown()`; the second one says "console: the line history: 4 lines kept from before the stop", Up and Up give the line and Enter runs it (18), and Control-R `kept` finds it.
52. The extended state of the processor (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The extended state of the processor"). `kerneltool test extended_state` starts three machines. With `-cpu max` the log has "kernel: extended state: x87, SSE, AVX; one save writes at most 832 bytes, in an area of 2688 bytes in each thread entry; saved at a switch of threads with xsaveopt"; the tool reads the host (`avx512f`, `avx512bw` in `/proc/cpuinfo`, and `/dev/kvm`) and waits for the line with "AVX-512" and 2688 bytes when the host gives it. The program `extended_state` starts as many threads as processors plus two (31 at most); each holds ymm0 to ymm15 (or zmm0 to zmm31 and k0 to k7, 64 bits of each with AVX-512 BW), the x87 stack, the x87 control word and `MXCSR` with a pattern of its own for each pass, across 16 kernel entries of each pass (half of them give the processor away), for 3 s; in round 5 only `MXCSR` changes, in round 10 only the x87 control word; it checks each register and the start state of the thread (picture `extended_state`). With `-cpu max,-avx` the kernel saves x87 and SSE with `xsaveopt` (576 bytes) and the program holds xmm0 to xmm15 (picture `extended_state_without_avx`). With `-cpu max,-xsave` the kernel saves with `fxsave` (512 bytes). `kerneltool measure` prints one empty kernel entry with xmm, ymm and zmm in use. Host tests: `support/instruction_sets` (the usable instruction sets from `CPUID` and `XCR0`). Measured by the coordinator on the machine without `xsave`: "6 threads on 4 processors, 2250 passes, 36000 kernel entries", an empty kernel entry 142 ns with xmm in use. Since the change of 2026-11-12 (550), a kernel entry saves nothing: the threads of `extended_state` hold their registers through each entry with no save, which checks that the kernel itself changes none, and through each switch to another thread of the test on the same processor (each wait of an odd round leaves the processor to one of the other threads: there are two more threads than processors). The build refuses a kernel with an x87, MMX, SSE or AVX instruction outside the save and the restore (551). `kerneltool measure` also prints one hand-over between two threads by the word wait with x87 and SSE in use and with the ymm high halves in use, and the cycles of each instruction of the save and the restore, measured by the program on its own area. Measured by the coordinator (`kerneltool measure`, two runs of each build, host load 6.4 to 8.7): kernel entries in one second with one locker 8 063 360 and 8 359 466 (124 and 120 ns) against 5 669 546 and 5 613 013 on `master` (176 and 178 ns); with 4 lockers 26 945 066 and 27 561 173 against 20 326 826 and 20 503 040; one hand-over between two threads 128 and 125 ns with x87 and SSE in use, 133 and 127 ns with the ymm high halves in use.
53. The return to a program (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The return to a program"). `kerneltool test return_fault` starts one machine. While `extended_state` holds its registers in more threads than processors, the program `return_fault` starts three children, each with a thread whose return the processor would refuse at `iretq`: a `syscall` in the last two bytes below `USER_END` (return address 2^47, not canonical; the thread holds values in the high halves of its ymm registers), a stack address that is not canonical held while the thread waits for the timer, and the flag nested task set with `popfq` before a `syscall`. The log has "kernel: program return_fault child stopped: the kernel cannot return to its thread: the instruction address is not an address of a program: 0000800000000000" and the same for "the stack address is not canonical: 8000000000000000"; the stop reports have the code `STOP_CODE_RETURN` (0x400) and the address; the third child returns with its flags less nested task and stops itself with the code 1. "return_fault: ok, 3 cases" and "extended_state: ok" in the same machine: the machine goes on, and each register of each thread of `extended_state` is kept (picture `return_fault`). By reading, before this change the first case and the third stop the machine (code 2): not run on the kernel before the change. After the independent read: five cases, also the trap flag set with `popfq` right before a `syscall` (the child stops on its own debug exception, `STOP_CODE_FAULT + 1`, and the machine goes on) and a stack address that is not canonical held at a `syscall`; and a second machine that boots `kernel_without_return_check`, a kernel of the test that skips the check of the instruction address: the processor refuses the `iretq` of the child of the instruction address, and the second layer stops the child ("the processor refused the return, with the instruction address 0000800000000000").
54. The fault ring (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The fault ring"). `kerneltool test fault_ring` starts one machine; the program `fault_ring` runs one child for each role. The child that repairs names a handler thread: a read of a page that is not committed is repaired by a commit, and the thread reads the value that the handler wrote ("the thread read 5eed000000000003: as it must"); a write to a read-only page is repaired by `Memory_Protect`; a stack grows by 64 pages, one repaired fault each ("one repaired fault took ___ ns at least", the least of 64); an answer with a wrong serial is refused with `Bad_State`. The handler answers `Stop`: the stop code 0x300 of a page that is not committed and the address, as with no handler. A fault of the handler itself: the same stop. A full ring (17 threads fault, the handler reads none): the 17th fault stops the program, and the kernel writes "the fault ring is full (1 times)". The program stops while a thread waits, and the handler ends while a thread waits (the stop code 0x500). Then: a thread whose last word wait left its entry in the table of words faults, and the page of that word is decommitted while it waits: no second record, and one answer resumes it; a new handler while a thread waits stops the program with 0x500; an answer from another program with the number and the serial of the record is refused with `Bad_State`; 10 000 faults answered at once by a handler that spins on another processor resume 10 000 times, each with the right value ("10000 faults resumed, 10000 values right, of 10000: as it must, in ___ ms"). The parent waits for the stop of each child at most the hang bound (60 s) with no progress, and 600 s in all: the child of the 10 000 faults writes its count of faults after each one, and each growth starts the 60 s again; a child that writes no progress has 60 s. At a timeout, the log gives the step, the count of answers and the time ("no progress for 60000 ms at step ___ of 10000, ___ answers, after ___ ms"). The case of a word wait and a fault starts the thread that waits, and wakes it only when `Thread_State` says that it waits. Then the fault kinds that are not page faults, each in a child whose handler asks for its kind: a divide error, an invalid opcode (`ud2`) and a privileged instruction (`cli`, a protection fault) each give one record of their kind with the error code 0 and the instruction of the stop report; the handler answers `Resume`, which the kernel refuses with `Bad_State`, then `Stop`, which gives the stop code of the vector ("1 record of the kind 0, the error code 0, the instruction of the stop report; Resume got Bad_State: as it must"); a breakpoint (`int3`, a trap) answered `Resume` lets the thread go on after it ("computed 42: as it must"); a handler of page faults only does not get a divide error, and the program stops as with no handler ("the handler read 0 records"); `Fault_Handler_Set` refuses 14 vectors that the kernel never delivers and kinds with the end of the delivery ("refused 15 arguments of 15"), and a divide error of the handler itself stops its program. Each child gives back every page, every thread and its program to the budget of the parent. Then the control of a thread, cases 20 to 27 in one child: a stop of a thread that spins finds its instruction address inside its loop, and the loop does not turn while it is stopped; a write of r12 is published by the thread after its `Resume`; four frames that a program cannot hold and the hostile extended states are refused with `Bad_Operation`, and the thread runs after; a thread stopped in a word wait is woken and does not run until its `Resume`; two stops need two resumes, and a third `Resume` gets `Bad_State`; a handle without `Control` gets `No_Right`; a divide error whose handler moves `rip` past the `div` and writes `rax` goes on with 42, where `Resume` before the write got `Bad_State`. Case 28: a child names no thread of the parent (32 calls refused), and the thread of the parent keeps running with the stop count 0. Cases 29 to 33, one child whose handler takes `Debug`, `Breakpoint` and `General_Protection`: `int 3` in two bytes is a breakpoint at +2; `int 14` is a protection fault with the error code 0x72, which the handler skips with a write of `rip`; `int1` is a debug trap at +1; the trap flag set with `popfq` gives 6 debug records; `mov ss` then `syscall` with the trap flag: the machine goes on, and each record is a debug trap in the code of the program. Picture `fault_ring` (33 rows in two columns; the row of the stack shows the time of one repaired fault).

## The path from power to desktop

1. The firmware starts the boot program (`boot/`), a PE file.
2. The boot program reads the kernel module and the boot image from the boot volume. It loads the kernel with the code of `contract/module/`. It collects the memory map and the framebuffer in a `Boot_Info` record, leaves the firmware, and jumps to the kernel.
3. The kernel (`kernel/`) takes the memory, builds its tables, sets the processor tables, the interrupt controller and the timer. It reads the list of the processors in the ACPI tables and starts each one.
4. The kernel loads one program from the boot image: the root service. It gives it three handles: the machine, the framebuffer, the boot image.
5. The root service (`programs/root`) loads the other programs in user space with `system.program_start`. It starts the input service, the compositor and the console.
   The boot display (`support/bootscreen/`) is on the screen from step 2 to step 5. The boot program draws it with what it knows. The root service draws it again before each start, with the records of the kernel. The compositor then takes the screen.
6. From this point, the kernel only executes kernel operations, schedules, and turns interrupts into wakes.

## Kernel

| Subject | Now |
|---|---|
| Entry | One entry point: the `syscall` instruction. `rdi` is 0, a deadline in milliseconds, or `WAIT_FOREVER`. `rax` returns the time |
| Kernel operations | 26 command kinds in the kernel ring of each thread. `contract/operation/operation.odin` is the full list |
| Objects | Memory, Program, Thread, Interrupt, Ports, Machine, Budget, Log |
| Budget | A budget holds pages, programs and threads. `Memory_Create`, `Program_Create` and `Thread_Create` take a budget handle with the right Spend. The budget pays for memory objects, thread areas, page tables and slots. `Budget_Create` moves a part of a budget to a new budget. What an object used returns to the budget that paid. A budget with no handle ends: what it holds returns to its parent, and its parent pays for its objects. The kernel creates one budget at boot, for the root service: all the free pages and all the slots. The sum of the pages of all budgets is always the count of free pages; `System_Report` gives both numbers |
| Log | `Log` takes a Log handle with the right Write. The root service gives it to each program that it starts |
| Rights | Each handle slot holds a set of rights: Give, Read, Write, Execute, Control, Inspect, Devices, Stop. The handle of a new object has each right of its kind. `Handle_Give(target, handle, Rights)` copies a handle with the rights that are in both sets. No operation adds a right. An operation on a handle without the needed right gives `No_Right` |
| Tables | Flat and fixed: 2048 memory objects, 64 programs, 256 threads, 128 handles and 128 mappings per program, 32 threads per program |
| Threads | A thread is a register state and a thread area: the thread record and the kernel ring of the thread. `Thread_Create(entry, stack, parameter)` adds a thread to the caller. The threads of a program share its memory and its handles. A program with no thread stops with code 0 |
| Memory | A memory object is pages with a frame list and a reference count. `Memory_Create` reserves: the budget pays for each page now, and each page gets its zero page frame at its first use, by a page fault or by the kernel. `Memory_Map` maps it in a program. Device memory, thread areas and the first program are contiguous |
| Wait | A thread waits for a time, or on words (the word wait). The kernel has no other wake. For an interrupt, the stop of a held program and the end of another thread, the kernel adds 1 to the event word of each thread of the program, and wakes the threads that wait on that word |
| Word wait | `Word_Wait(address of Word records, count)` and `Word_Wake(address, count)`. The kernel names a word by its page frame, so the word can be in memory that two programs share. One entry can wait on 64 words. `system.Lock` and the wait of a ring are built on it |
| Scheduler | One run queue for each processor. Three strategies, each as six procedures that the kernel calls through a `switch` on the strategy of the queue: round robin, fair (virtual time and weights) and EEVDF (the strategy at boot); a program with the right Schedule changes the strategy while the machine runs (`Strategy_Set`). Wake preemption, also on another processor. A time slice of 10 ms is the longest turn. A woken thread goes to the queue of the processor where it ran last; an idle processor, or one with less load, takes a thread from another queue. Idle is `hlt`: a processor that makes a thread ready wakes an idle processor with an interrupt |
| Processors | At most 64. Each processor has a processor record: its kernel stack, its task state with the port bitmap, its segment table, its current thread, its counts. No lock covers all the kernel: a lock protects one kind of data (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Locks of the kernel"). Each processor has its own timer at 1000 Hz (local APIC); the first processor counts the time. The 16 device lines stay on the 8259 and interrupt the first processor |
| Address translations | Before the kernel frees a memory object or a page table, each other processor that uses it passes a point where it reloads its table (`processor_sync`). A running thread is never taken from its processor: the processor ends it |
| Devices | A Ports object opens a port range in the port bitmap of the program. An Interrupt object counts interrupts and wakes its owner: a line of the 8259, or a message interrupt with a vector of its own. Device memory is a memory object over the registers of a device, mapped with no cache. `Memory_Physical` gives the physical address of a memory object to the holder of the right Physical |
| Faults | A fault in a thread stops its program with a code. The kernel wakes each program that holds its handle |
| Life of a program | A program runs while a handle names it. When its last handle closes, the kernel stops it. Only the root service has no handle |
| Machine | One Machine object with four rights. Inspect: read `System_Report`, `Program_Report` and `Processor_Report` records. Devices: create device handles. Stop: stop the machine.
